[2081] It shows the dependence on the aforementioned organisms

[2082] The first type of body is made up of ministerial undersecretariats and branches of the Armed Forces, which are financed exclusively through fiscal contributions and make transfers to fund other defense programs

[2083] The second type of agency corresponds to entities directly dependent on the undersecretariats; this category also includes programs carried out by Armed Forces organizations that provide services to third parties and are financed by the funds they collect (own revenue

[2084] These include health services provided by military institutions or those providing services to society, such as the DGTMN or the DGAC

[2085] The third type consists of defense sector institutions that represent separate budget chapters and coordinate with the Ministry for these purposes through the undersecretariats

[2086] These organizations are funded by resources generated by the services they provide and are supplemented in some cases by fiscal contributions (IGM, SHOA and FAF

[2087] In the case of the Carabineros and the Investigative Police, although they are part of the Ministry of Defense, their budgets correspond to public security, not defense

[2088] It is important to note that, from a budgetary perspective, the National Defense Pension Fund (CAPREDENA) is not part of the Ministry of National Defense, but rather part of the Ministry of Labor and Social Security

[2089] Furthermore, given that the Armed Forces obtain part of their funding directly through the Reserved Copper Law, it is important to note that these resources are not included in the Ministry's budget or recorded in public accounting—they are, in any case, reported in public finance statistics under extra-budgetary expenditures—and that the system for programming and controlling them is subject to different regulations

[2090] Budgetary Procedures The General Budget of the Nation is the State's central tool for the allocation of public resources

[2091] Its definition takes into account all technically and economically feasible policies, so that within the set of economic and financial capacities established by fiscal policy, those to which the political authority gives priority are financed

[2092] The authority that must propose the Budget is the President of the Republic, and this project must be approved by the National Congress in the form of a law that governs for an annual budget period

[2093] The Minister of National Defense exercises overall coordination and direction in the implementation of fiscal and budgetary policies in the defense sector and is responsible for submitting budgets for administrative and legislative processing

[2094] The Minister of National Defense also assumes oversight of the budgetary management of the entire defense sector, regardless of the actions taken by each of the defense institutions according to their constitutional organic laws or other legal systems

[2095] With respect to each of the institutions of the Armed Forces and other bodies under their jurisdiction, the ministerial body responsible for submitting budgets is the respective Undersecretary, with the Undersecretary required to participate in the preparation of the budget and report on the progress of budget execution to the Comptroller General of the Republic and the Budget Directorate of the Ministry of Finance

[2096] Regarding the budget management of the Armed Forces, the Commanders-in-Chief are responsible for the formulation and execution of budgets for the various programs, in accordance with their constitutional organic law

[2097] Within the organizational structure of each institution, there are entities responsible for preparing the corresponding budget, carrying out its execution, and ensuring control

[2098] The Armed Forces have separate systems for planning, budget development, financial execution, and management evaluation

[2099] These systems are framed within the general regulations governing the State's financial administration and, like all other public administration bodies, are subject to the administrative and accounting oversight carried out by the Comptroller General of the Republic and to the budgetary oversight carried out by the Budget Directorate

[2100] The resource allocation system described is designed to allow the various priorities of the defense sector to converge and be combined in the preparation of the General Budget of the Nation

[2101] In this process, defense priorities will be evaluated based on their technical, economic, and political characteristics and made compatible with the priorities of the other sectors of the State

[2102] According to the bill modernizing the Ministry of National Defense, approved by Congress in December 2009, long-term budget planning and arms purchases approved by the Executive Branch in the year corresponding to the fiscal year in question must be reported to Congress

[2103] Reserved Copper Law No. 13,196 of 1958, known as the Reserved Copper Law (LRC), aims to finance the acquisition of weapons and equipment systems, that is, the acquisition and maintenance of the materials and elements that make up the national war potential

[2104] This law establishes a 10% tax on the value of copper and copper derivatives exports made by the Copper Corporation (Codelco

[2105] It also sets a minimum amount equivalent to US$180,000,000 (one hundred and eighty million dollars), adjusted for the variation in the United States Wholesale Price Index (WPI) between 1987 and the year in question

[2106] For years in which 10% of Codelco's exports fall short of this value, the law requires the General Treasury of the Republic to deposit the difference into the corresponding accounts created for the purposes of this Law

[2107] Until 2003, the Treasury deposited the proceeds of this tax in equal parts into reserved accounts assigned to each of the Armed Forces institutions

[2108] These, in turn, constituted an amount close to 5% in an account managed by the Higher Council of National Defense (CONSUDENA), intended for joint projects

[2109] Since 2003, however, the minimum amount guaranteed by law has been deposited into institutional accounts, and all funds earned above that minimum have been allocated to the account managed by CONSUDENA

[2110] Given the confidential nature of the law, both the value of the funds delivered to the armed forces and their destination are restricted; reference values can be obtained from Codelco's balance sheets

[2111] This legal system has guaranteed a long-term flow of resources to the Armed Forces, ensuring them a minimum annual income

[2113] DIPRES – Ministry of Finance. Since 2003, the incorporation of an accrual basis into the Chilean accounting system has meant that fiscal contributions do not necessarily coincide with expenditures

[2114] Furthermore, the inclusion of the actual expenditure of resources generated by the Copper Law in fiscal statistics has made it unnecessary to resort to the law's performance criterion

[2115] The trend toward differentiating between fiscal contributions and expenditures has become particularly evident in recent years, given that the resources generated by this legal instrument are spent in a controlled manner

[2116] In this sense, the political authority's circumspection in approving the allocation of resources from the Copper Law has restricted the use of this law despite the increased returns obtained as a result of the increase in the international price of copper and its derivatives

[2117] However, reasons of economic efficiency made it advisable to look for elements that would allow for more flexible policies known as "thirds

[2118] As a way to achieve this goal, the Executive Branch submitted a request to the Comptroller General's Office through the Ministry of Defense

[2119] In December 2002, the Comptroller's Office issued a ruling determining that the funds allocated to the various Reserve Accounts may be used to finance investment projects of any of the Armed Forces institutions, provided that said projects have the support of all the Commanders-in-Chief

[2120] This made the use of funds generated by the RLC more flexible, eliminating one of the existing inefficiencies in this source of financing

[2121] BILL ON A NEW NATIONAL DEFENSE FINANCING SYSTEM The enactment of the Reserved Copper Law in 1958 responded to the need to generate a stable, non-contingent flow of financial resources for defense infrastructure projects and the acquisition of weapons systems

[2122] Since its enactment in 1958, the law has undergone several modifications, as well as administrative adjustments in its application

[2123] The most significant administrative adjustment was introduced in 2003, when the distribution method for the resources generated through this method was changed, their use was made more flexible, and their actual expenditure was limited

[2124] The country's development, the modernization of the State, and the evolution of our budgetary institutions made it advisable to replace this special law with a defense financing system that would eliminate the existence of a special law for this purpose

[2125] In response to this objective, the Executive Branch submitted a bill to the National Congress in September 2009, thus initiating parliamentary debate and the process of legislative approval

[2126] The Constitutional Organic Law of the Armed Forces establishes minimum values for the different contributions established in each fiscal year

[2127] These minimum values were formulated on the basis of the budget executed in 1989 and they establish that the contribution for salaries in national currency must be adjusted at least in accordance with the general adjustments for the public sector and the contribution for the rest of the expenses in national currency must be adjusted according to the variation of the CPI between the year in question and

[2128] Chart 58 shows the evolution of these contributions between 1990 and 2008 separately, showing a growth of 62% for staff and 58% for others in both categories

[2129] CHART 58 FISCAL CONTRIBUTION TO FF. AA

[2130] Regarding the contribution in foreign currency, the law establishes that it cannot be less than that corresponding to the year 1989, without establishing a readjustment mechanism

[2131] The annual contribution in nominal dollars between 1990 and 2008, illustrated in Chart 59, shows an increase of 42% over that period

[2146] AIR (25.02%) 2008 ARMY (41.55%) Composition From the point of view of its composition, defense spending is divided into five large areas: Personnel, Operations, Equipment, Infrastructure and Research and Development

[2147] While budget expenditures can be separated into the categories indicated, those financed with funds from the Reserved Copper Law cannot be separated in the same manner

[2148] These include operational expenses, construction of military infrastructure, equipment, and associated financial expenses

[2149] However, given that the largest component of this expenditure has been the acquisition of military equipment, all of the resources generated from this source are classified as "war material

[2150] The structure of defense spending still maintains much of what determined its distribution and use during the second half of the 20th century

[2151] That is, a strong emphasis on personnel spending, low or reduced operating costs, and facilities that are still in transition and require retrofitting

[2152] A special case is Research and Development, a concept that is not separated in public accounts, which is why it is included in the other expenses

[2153] Chart 61 shows the distribution of expenditure by these criteria for the years 1994 and

[2154] The relative increase in the Personnel item and the relative decrease in expenditure on Operations and Infrastructure items can be seen, which is explained by real increases in staff salaries that are proportionally greater than the increases in the rest of the operating expenses

[2155] This choice regarding staff compensation must be understood in the context of the growth, up to 2008, in the income levels of the population throughout the country

[2156] COMPOSITION OF THE FISCAL CONTRIBUTION TO DEFENSE (in thousands of 2008 pesos) Until recently, the Chilean Armed Forces were operating equipment that was in the final stages of its useful life, and therefore had a comparatively high maintenance cost

[2157] Furthermore, the moderate growth in operating expenditure is evidence that emphasis has been placed on maintaining the forces' operational capabilities

[2158] It should be noted that the process of modernizing and replacing the equipment acquired between the 1970s and 1980s, much of which had a long operational life at the time of purchase, began in 1997 with the acquisition of two new submarines and refurbished second-hand battle tanks

[2159] Spending on military infrastructure is low, reflecting the fact that there have been no substantial changes in the distribution and number of units, with existing infrastructure being used and renovated

[2160] The Armed Forces' modernization projects, both for replacing end-of-life equipment and for restructuring the forces, will mean that investment in this area will increase in the coming years

[2161] This effort will have to be financed, in large part, through the consolidation of existing infrastructure

[2162] Contributions According to Their Purpose The fiscal contribution to defense is delivered with specific objectives derived from the budgetary methodology, which allows it to be classified according to the type of expense it must finance: "Personnel" and "Other" in national currency

[2163] The first corresponds to contributions to finance salaries and other personnel expenses; the second finances operational and real investment expenses (primarily in infrastructure

[2164] The main purpose of the foreign currency contribution is to finance other operational expenses; it also finances military missions abroad, which manage equipment purchases and the expenses of military attachés

[2165] In addition to the fiscal contributions described above, there are expenses financed by the Reserved Copper Law, which are intended for the acquisition of military equipment

[2166] Chart 62 shows the evolution of these contributions, presenting their distribution in the years 1990, 1996, 2002 and

[2167] It shows a significant variation in the composition of contributions, with the personal component having risen from 41% in 1990 to 55% in

[2168] Starting this year, the trend began to stabilize, and since 2001, it has begun to decline slightly as a result of layoffs aimed at restructuring the personnel structure

[2169] This downward trend is expected to become more pronounced in the coming years

[2170] GRAPH 62 FISCAL CONTRIBUTION ACCORDING TO ITS DESTINATION The relative weight of the Reserved Copper Law falls in the same period from 35.5% in 1990, to a figure that fluctuates around 21% in the rest of the period

[2171] This variation corresponds to the significant fluctuations in the yield of the grade, primarily due to the international price of copper

[2172] The other contributions have not had major variations and have maintained a relatively stable participation

[2173] Personnel Expenditure and its Trend In general, the Armed Forces plants have experienced two situations associated with and derived from the high number of personnel entered during the 70s in the context of the external challenges experienced during that decade (chart

[2174] The first situation is the distortion of the personnel pyramids, a phenomenon that occurs because fewer classes graduated from the parent schools in the 1980s and 1990s

[2175] The second phenomenon is the so-called “aging of the plant,” caused by the high average number of years of service in the workforce, which leads to higher costs

[2176] This latter situation became evident starting in 1994, with the increase in staff benefits linked to professional development

[2177] Added to these two phenomena is the fact that in recent years there was a general increase in public sector salaries due to salary increases above the CPI

[2178] In addition, permanent personnel have received special pay increases: in 1996, a salary increase was granted, and the Armed Forces Health Law was enacted, which included a compensatory bonus for the increase in health care contributions; in 1997, an increase was granted in association with the enactment of the new Armed Forces Personnel Statute (DFL-1 of 1997), and in 1999, a non-taxable bonus was created, resulting in another pay increase

[2179] Finally, in 2009, a salary increase was granted to all Armed Forces personnel, along with special bonuses for some strategic roles, such as pilots and commandos assigned to operational units

[2180] The situation described allows us to appreciate the challenges that the sector's personnel policies face

[2181] To address these challenges, both the Armed Forces' modernization processes and the improvement of resource allocation systems promoted by the Ministry of Defense include instruments and objectives aimed at streamlining and improving human resource management

[2182] Finally, the creation of a professional troop system made up of professional soldiers hired for a five-year term entails a much higher cost than maintaining a similar number of conscripts—both in terms of pay and living conditions, and above all, training—which introduces new concepts into the Armed Forces' expenditure structure and personnel management

[2183] The defense investment system is the framework for analyzing all investment initiatives in the national defense sector

[2184] This system has a methodology that includes a decision-making flow structure, as well as standards and procedures for the presentation and evaluation of defense investment projects

[2185] In this way, the system complements the role played by the National Investment System (SNI) of the Ministry of Planning (MIDEPLAN) in the public sector, from which the defense sector is excluded

[2186] The development of the defense investment system is a synthesis of work carried out by the Ministry of National Defense, with the participation of representatives from each branch of the Armed Forces, the National Defense General Staff (EMDN), the National Defense Council (CONSUDENA), the Ministry of Planning (MIDEPLAN), the Budget Directorate (DIPRES) of the Ministry of Finance, and the Advisory Committee of the Minister of National Defense

[2187] The fundamental objective of the system, already in operation, is to ensure that defense sector investments are incorporated into the country's public investment strategy, within the context of the joint planning and development of the Armed Forces

[2188] This, with the aim of achieving greater transparency, efficiency, and effectiveness in the management of defense investment resources

[2189] Investment projects, prepared and evaluated by the institutions of the Armed Forces and the National Defense Staff (EMDN), are presented by the respective institutional Commanders-in-Chief and the Head of the EMDN to the Minister of National Defense

[2190] If deemed appropriate, the Minister shall forward these pre-investment studies to the relevant Ministry of Defense agency for evaluation and issuance of a report containing the results of the technical and economic evaluation

[2191] The decision-making process considers three stages, associated with the development phases of a project and therefore the corresponding pre-investment studies: profile, pre-feasibility, and feasibility

[2192] In terms of content, these pre-investment studies are identical; the difference between them lies in the quality and detail of their data and information, and the depth of their analysis: The Profile study may be based on technical and economic information from suppliers at the Rough Order of Magnitude (ROM) level or from other sources

[2193] • In the Pre-feasibility stage, the source of information for the study must correspond, at least, to information obtained from a “Request for Information” (RFI) document

[2194] • The feasibility study will require sources of information with greater technical and economic certainty than the pre-feasibility study, in order to have the necessary information to make a decision with the widest range of options

[2195] Not all projects, however, must sequentially go through each and every instance of the decision flow before reaching a decision on the project, including the decision to approve the project

[2196] The evaluation report submitted by the Ministry's reviewing-evaluating body will contain the recommendations submitted to the Minister

[2197] The decision of this authority on the study of the project in question will be communicated to the submitting institution by means of a letter accompanied by the corresponding evaluation report

[2198] Institutional Preparation and Presentation of Investment Projects The preparation that military institutions make of an investment project aims to collect and analyze the background and information that allow justifying the execution of said project

[2199] Based on the information available, the institution in question may prepare profile, pre-feasibility, or feasibility studies

[2200] In addition to the executive summary, each pre-investment study must have a minimum structure of background, information, analysis, evaluation, and recommendation

[2201] The Institutional Evaluation Process

[2202] Technical evaluation The operational effectiveness of each solution alternative is evaluated through the criteria of performance and operational availability, using a multi-criteria analysis methodology approach called "Hierarchical Analytical Process

[2203] Operational performance is defined as the ability to meet defined objectives, considering the integration of all its subsystems

[2204] In turn, operational availability is defined as the ability to be available to meet defined objectives

[2205] The breakdown of these criteria, to define the entire decision-making model, is specific to each project

[2206] b. Economic evaluation

[2207] Economic evaluation is only applicable to those alternatives that have been selected in the technical evaluation, that is, those that meet the minimum requirements in terms of operational effectiveness

[2208] For this evaluation, the cost-benefit or cost-efficiency approach or criterion is adopted, depending on whether or not it is possible to quantify the project's benefits in monetary terms

[2209] When the costs and benefits of the project can be expressed in monetary terms, the cost-benefit approach or criterion will be adopted, with the Net Present Value (NPV) and/or Internal Rate of Return (IRR) indicators

[2210] In this situation, the decision criterion corresponds to that of maximum benefit

[2211] When it is not possible to express the benefits of the project in monetary terms, or when the effort to achieve them has a high cost, the cost-efficiency approach or criterion will be adopted, with its indicators Present Value of Costs (VAC) or Annual Equivalent Cost (CAE), depending on whether the alternatives have the same or different useful lives

[2212] In this case, the decision criterion corresponds to the minimum cost, calculated by one of the aforementioned indicators

[2213] Project Life Cycle Analysis

[2214] This is shown in chart 64: LIFE CYCLE OF AN INVESTMENT PROJECT Idea Evaluation Tender and Award Materialization of the Investment Operation and Maintenance Withdrawal / Retirement Disposal Pre-investment 0 I II Investment Operation PHASES III IV V STAGES The work developed by the defense investment system corresponds to the “Evaluation” stage of the “Pre-investment” phase

[2215] For the "Investment" phase, work has been done to establish uniform criteria and standardize procedures applicable to institutional decision-making in the acquisition of weapons systems and war material

[2216] In order to have a complete view of the project lifecycle, control elements will need to be incorporated into the system in the "Operation" phase in the future

[2217] This involves, among other actions, the introduction of ex post evaluations, that is, the task of comparing the effects generated by a project with the objectives expressed in pre-investment studies

[2218] The consolidation of the defense investment system entails reviewing the functioning of the structures generated to date by the implementation of the system, both within the Ministry of Defense and in military institutions and the EMDN, introducing any necessary modifications

[2219] This process will involve implementing the computer systems designed and developed to provide IT support for the pre-investment phase of projects, using a platform with the highest security standards

[2220] Comprehensive Evaluation of Defense Investments Although the current Defense Investment Project Evaluation System represents a significant advance within the defense sector, the evaluations of each project are carried out independently, based on the merits of each project, even when all elements of consistency with the joint development of the sector are incorporated

[2221] An important future task is the analysis, design, and development of methodological elements for conducting evaluations that, when integrated into a joint capabilities development plan, will allow us to determine the greater or lesser impact of each project on the overall needs established for the country's defense. 21st Century

[2222] Other Innovations in the Defense Sector As noted, the resources needed to maintain the country's defense capability originate mainly from two laws: the National Budget Law and the Reserved Copper Law

[2223] Regarding the resources allocated annually through the Budget Law, the Ministry of Defense has developed a series of administrative and financial policies and mechanisms to improve their allocation and use within the context of a financial transparency policy implemented since

[2224] The Budget Information System In this context, in the last five years there has been significant progress in consolidating information on the budget chapters that comprise the defense sector, allowing for greater knowledge and monitoring of the structure and execution of the budget for each of them

[2225] Through a series of communication mechanisms with and between the various institutional branches and organizations linked to the defense sector, as well as the integration of subjects and criteria with a shared vision, consolidated information has been generated under guiding principles shared by all sectoral stakeholders

[2226] In this way, the sectoral budget information system collects monthly reports on the progress of the execution of the resources allocated to each of the services that comprise the Ministry of Defense

[2227] Through this system, the Executive Branch can constantly evaluate the sector's activities and, if necessary, correct deviations from the medium- and long-term investment project plans developed. It also supports the Minister of Defense's management and decision-making process in this regard

[2228] It should be noted that the sectoral budget information system complements the work of the Defense Investment Project Evaluation System, which leads to the implementation of approved projects, i.e., their provision of resources and their execution through the budget

[2229] Budget Management refers to the set of mechanisms and tools for the efficient allocation of resources for the sector, as well as for periodically evaluating and controlling their correct execution and programming of the use of said resources, which the Ministry of Defense carries out in a centralized and consolidated manner

[2230] This process aligns the macroeconomic assumptions of fiscal policy with the objectives of defense policy in relation to sectoral modernization processes

[2231] For these purposes, budget management is implemented at various levels: At the formulation level: proper compliance with the instructions for submitting the budget framework and requests for additional resources is ensured; the draft budgets of each of the sector's institutions are consolidated and centralized, ensuring the quality and timeliness of information submitted to the Budget Directorate of the Ministry of Finance

[2232] At the discussion level: the correct interpretation of the resource-allocating agency is ensured, both with respect to the carrying-over activities, which generally follow a long-term policy and, in their annual component, are executed according to the established program, as well as new initiatives that require additional financing

[2233] This stage of the management process is supported by project financial reports and evaluation reports for each project

[2234] At the execution level: the timely and effective use of resources is ensured through the systems, equipment, and processes that the Ministry of Defense has developed

[2235] • At the evaluation level: Programs and their effectiveness are evaluated according to the terms defined in the designs and proposals submitted to the Executive, in order to ensure continuity of said programs and establish standards for measuring their compliance

[2236] The incorporation of advanced technologies into the military requires technological development that reflects a national capacity to exploit and sustain the incorporated systems

[2237] Consequently, technological development is a fundamental component for the credibility of deterrence

[2238] There are many examples in Latin America where large quantities of military equipment have been incorporated into the forces without obtaining advanced combat capabilities due to a lack of technological development

[2239] Technological development is a matter of capabilities, not products, although these are ultimately the consequence of having such capabilities

[2240] These capabilities are scientific in nature, that is, they are the set of skills and abilities—both technical and managerial—that allow us to move from identifying a need to providing an economically viable solution to meet that need

[2241] RESOURCES APPLIED IN SCIENCE, TECHNOLOGY AND INDUSTRY

[2242] The promulgation of the National Defense Science and Technology Policy and the creation of the Higher Council for Science and Technology are two important milestones that support the inter-institutional coordination, development, and strengthening of scientific and technological research activities

[2243] These initiatives help position science, technology, and innovation as key factors in developing the strength of national defense institutions

[2244] It also allows for the coordination of efforts by various ministerial bodies in the promotion, development, and financing of science and technology projects in the Army, Navy, and Air Force

[2245] Vision: Research in science and technology carried out by institutions must consider technological solutions appropriate to the specific needs of force development

[2246] However, these scientific developments must satisfy both military and civilian requirements, through technology transfers to public or private sectors

[2247] In the area of knowledge, there is a looming gap between what will be the most advanced defense technology globally and what is available to our country

[2248] Consequently, to bridge this gap, cooperation between defense institutions and national universities and technology companies must be encouraged

[2249] Technological Development Objectives The following are the technological development objectives to be achieved by the armed institutions to materialize the science and technology policy of national defense: Research and development capabilities Promote an organizational and legal infrastructure that allows generating capabilities in this area by making efficient use of the resources available in the national defense sector

[2250] Research and development links Promote links to generate synergies between research and development efforts in science and technology carried out in the defense sector and those carried out by other national entities, public and private, or by institutions in other countries

[2251] • Human capital development: Increase the level of professional training of defense personnel, as well as develop a critical mass of experts in the scientific field, in general, and in military technologies of interest, in particular

[2252] • Capacity building in the defense industry Propose to the national industry the capacity building to support, throughout their life cycle, the systems and equipment incorporated into institutions, thus reducing dependence on external suppliers, especially in the areas of optronics, automatic control, communications, ammunition, electronics, and command and control

[2253] • Increased development: Generate strategic alliances between state-owned companies in the defense sector and national or foreign entities

[2254] • National cartography Improve the capacity to generate national cartography through the Geocentric Reference System for the Americas (SIRGAS) georeferenced with digital technical databases shared between the Military Geographic Institute (IGM), the Hydrographic and Oceanographic Service of the Navy (SHOA) and the Aerophotogrammetric Service of the Air Force (SAF

[2255] Extended weather forecasts Improve the ability to generate automated weather forecasts from mathematical models, using satellite images and performing real-time analysis of climatological and oceanographic variables, with a minimum prediction threshold of 10 days

[2256] • Non-conventional renewable energy Incorporate and use non-conventional renewable energy (NCRE) in the activities of the Armed Forces, especially in isolated locations, to reduce dependence on fossil fuels

[2257] • Advanced materials Incorporate advanced materials (nanomaterials) for use in naval, military and air units with the purpose of reducing electromagnetic and acoustic footprints, as well as increasing resistance persistence in remote scenarios and survivability in demanding environments

[2258] • Satellite capacity (remote sensing) Contribute to the development of space capabilities based on the incorporation of specialized, high-tech, high-resolution satellites, in order to make communications and image generation services independent for military purposes and to support civilian activities

[2259] • Integrated command system: Develop a command and control system that integrates information generated by the various institutional defense entities. • Incorporation of cutting-edge technology: Incorporate advanced technologies in the areas of remote sensing, aeronautical systems, energy, simulation and modeling, and management

[2260] Activities of the Armed Forces Army In terms of science and technology, the Army has developed a series of activities, among which the following stand out: • The Computerized Tactical Training System (SETAC), which gave rise to the Tactical Operational Training Center (CEOTAC) and allowed for military and civilian training at the tactical and strategic level

[2261] • The SETAC WEB II System, which optimizes military training, the Emergency or Disaster Training and Management System (SIGEN), which allows for training with civilians dependent on the National Emergency Office of the Ministry of the Interior (ONEMI) and military personnel from various countries

[2262] • The Simulation System for Peacekeeping Operations (SIMUPAZ), which aims to train the Chilean and Argentine armies in peacekeeping operations, within the Combined Peacekeeping Force “Cruz del Sur.”

[2263] • The Army Modeling and Simulation Center, which aims to model and simulate the behavior of certain units on the battlefield

[2264] • The TIGO project, aimed at defining terrestrial reference points in time, space, and the Earth's gravitational field, is being promoted by the Chilean-German geodetic observatory in a joint effort between the Army, the University of Concepción, and the German Federal Agency for Cartography and Geodesy (BKG

[2265] ARMY Technology must meet requirements in the field of conducting operations, obtaining information and intelligence, and achieving intended effects on the ground

[2266] Specific technologies are identified in each of these areas

[2267] The above satisfies an institutional objective regarding the ground force, acting both in isolation and in a joint and combined environment

[2268] Finally, the Research and Control Institute (IDIC) serves as Chile's Test Bench, monitoring the safety of weapons, ammunition, pyrotechnics, explosives, and chemical substances used in the country

[2269] In addition, it maintains the "National Standard Custodian of Force" laboratory, as part of the "Chilean Metrology Network," through which the instruments used by the national industry are calibrated

[2270] By virtue of its functions, the IDIC advises the General Directorate of National Mobilization in the implementation of the "National System for Recording Ballistic Prints and Criminal Evidence

[2271] In the area of science and technology, the Navy is implementing a series of initiatives independently or in conjunction with institutions in the area of science and technological development

[2272] The foundations of technological development are derived from the Ocean Directive, which sets out the tasks imposed on the institution by both its mission and its responsibilities in the maritime field and defense policy

[2273] From the above, a development model has been adopted based on Protection Forces and Projection Forces; each of these forces has a development program with a horizon extending through

[2274] These projects are primarily aimed at improving operational and logistical management, as well as scientific knowledge of the marine environment. These include the following: • Developing renewable energy generation alternatives applicable to the Institution or in which the Navy has a significant stake and contribution (tidal, wind, solar

[2275] Develop energy efficiency optimization systems for floating units, land-based departments, ground transportation vehicles, and government offices. In conjunction with the Center for Scientific Studies (CECS), develop and operate an airborne radar system for tempered ice

[2276] To date, six ice measurement campaigns have been carried out on the Chilean mainland and in Antarctica

[2277] • In conjunction with Separately (Southeastern Pacific Research Institute, a multi-sector organization with the participation of national universities, government agencies, and foreign agencies

[2278] investigate the applicability of "nanotechnology" for the benefit of the Navy

[2279] Maintains and operates a tsunami warning system for authorities responsible for these types of national emergencies, as well as for the international tsunami warning system. Creates and updates electronic and paper charts for navigation in national waters, which are used by domestic and foreign vessels

[2280] • Monitoring of physical and chemical parameters in the ocean that affect the El Niño and Southern Oscillation phenomenon, which has a direct impact on the variations in meteorological conditions affecting the country

[2281] • Construction of a new scientific vessel with high-tech equipment, which will allow universities, scientific centers, researchers, and the Navy itself to delve deeper into marine research

[2282] • Optimization of asset management capacity through the widespread application of an ERP (Enterprise Resource Planning) system, known within the institution as the SALINO system, in an online environment with broad connectivity and which includes the development of a high-level institutional management system, providing up-to-date and reliable information to contribute to decision-making processes

[2283] Air Force In order to meet its needs and project the benefits of aerospace technologies towards the development of the country, the Air Force has developed a series of scientific and technological projects, among which the following stand out: • Development of the country's aerospace capacity, through the early development of the FASAT Alfa and FASAT Bravo satellites, which allowed the development of critical mass in this area

[2284] • Development of the Tactical Airbattle Control Management and Planning System (TACMAPS) operations planning system, in conjunction with the company “Development, Technology and System” (DTS

[2285] This system allows planning air operations in different scenarios

[2286] • The Aerofotogrammetric Service (SAF) of the Chilean Air Force delivers more than 24,000 frames per year and has contributed to various projects of aeronautical, economic and social impact, among which the following stand out: - Master Sample Frame of the National Institute of Statistics (INE) - Urban Cartography for Housing and Urban Planning Service (SERVIU) and Municipalities

[2287] Development of the country's Digital Aeronautical Cartography

[2288] Contribution to the development of Digital Aeronautical Cartography for Central America and the Caribbean, in conjunction with the International Civil Aviation Organization (ICAO

[2289] Various geophysical risk studies in the country

[2290] Contribution through soil studies to overcome extreme poverty, in conjunction with regional governments in various areas of the country

[2291] Geophysical studies in the Antarctic territory, in conjunction with the Chilean Antarctic Institute (INACH

[2292] • Participation in the Optics and Photonics Center at the University of Concepción, which will allow for developments in the areas of sensors, information security, and satellite image analysis. • Development of studies for the implementation of wind energy at the Presidente Eduardo Frei Montalva Antarctic Base, in conjunction with the University of Magallanes, the Federico Santa María Technical University, and the National Energy Commission

[2293] • Development of the VANT project at the Aeronautical Polytechnic Academy for the design, manufacture, and operation of an unmanned aerial vehicle

[2294] • Development of the Integrated Financial Management System (SAFI), which allows for efficient and secure financial management control

[2295] This system was adapted by the institution for use in the Illustrious Municipality of Easter Island

[2296] • Development of a Logistics Information System, which allows all logistics items to be integrated into a single computer platform, making management in this area more efficient

[2297] AIR FORCE The foundations of technological development are derived from the Bicentennial Objectives Plan and a horizon to the year

[2298] Based on this, three concepts have been defined: Gaining Strength, Gaining Knowledge, and Achieving Dominance; the first is associated with the year 2010, the second with 2015, and the third with 2020. The institutional areas to be addressed are: Air Bases, C4ISR, Air Defense, Support Aviation, and Combat Aviation

[2299] The Defense Industry The companies that make up the publicly owned defense industry in Chile have a similar legal status as autonomous state enterprises, which is expressed in their respective organic laws

[2300] Like any company, they must carry out their activities following the principle of economic rationality

[2301] This means that they are constantly challenged to optimize efficiency and economic performance, particularly return on invested capital and the productivity of the financial, material, and human resources assigned to their tasks

[2302] The application of this principle is particularly relevant in the defense industry, as these companies have strategic objectives for the country, as well as special implications for their decision-making processes at the management and executive levels

[2303] BOX 36: STRATEGIC OBJECTIVES OF PUBLIC ENTERPRISES The objectives that underpin the existence of publicly owned enterprises in Chile are the following: • Generate economic value: contributing wealth and direct income to the nation's budget

[2304] • Generate social profitability: implementing actions that provide a net benefit to society, but whose economic profitability is low and does not induce its materialization by the private sector

[2305] • Implement public policies: contributing to the country's development in a wide range of areas such as defense, territorial integration, production, market regulation, communications, transportation, and energy

[2306] Ultimately, these publicly owned military companies must develop their strategic planning in harmony with state policies that regulate the economy as a whole, including public and private business activity, and national security and defense, which consider, among other things, joint development and institutional plans

[2307] Current Situation The publicly owned military industry in Chile is closely linked to the origin and existence of the branches of the Armed Forces, and is therefore inherent to them

[2308] Its origin is directly related to decisions made at the time by the respective military institutions, which formed specialized entities to support their essential functions and meet maintenance and supply requirements for war material

[2309] Over time, military companies have achieved a significant level of business, industrial, and technological development, enabling them to meet the missions and objectives set by institutional authorities and thus meet the basic goods and services requirements of the Armed Forces

[2310] activities that have been complemented by the supply of goods and services oriented to the requirements of the civil or military world, national and foreign

[2311] Notwithstanding the above, in recent years the advisability of maintaining companies so directly linked to the Armed Forces or granting them corporate autonomy and integrating them into another organizational and control scheme, whether under state ownership or even by changing the ownership structure, has been a topic of public debate

[2312] In any case, given the challenges and high-tech requirements posed by the weapons systems acquired by the three Armed Forces institutions, defense companies have been subject to a comprehensive review process since

[2313] Although there is, formally, a general management and administration mechanism regulated by the organic laws that govern them, there are some differences among them in the treatment of issues such as their dependence and the instances of relationship with the government, the composition of the Councils or Boards of Directors, the powers or attributions to delegate of the Director of the Company, the process of accountability of the Director before the Council or Board of Directors and the powers that the Council or Board of Directors can exercise with respect to the creation or investment in related companies and distribution of profits

[2314] The differences noted make it advisable to move toward harmonizing business strategies and management criteria to optimize their management within the framework of the country's defense and military policies

[2315] Regarding their financial statements, companies have standardized the criteria for their review by external auditors in order to ensure adequate transparency and accountability in their financial, budgetary, and asset management

[2316] To date, sectoral ties to the management of defense companies are exercised through the participation of the Undersecretaries of War, Navy, and Air Force on the respective Boards or Boards of Directors, or through specific requests made by the Minister of Defense to the institutional Commanders-in-Chief

[2317] To refine the powers of the political authority and align its regulatory standards with those governing other public companies, defense industries have been included in the draft law on "corporate governance" currently in legislative proceedings

[2318] FAMAE, ASMAR and ENAER a. Army Factories and Workshops (FAMAE) In its mission to provide goods and services for national defense, FAMAE develops manufacturing, recovery and service projects in the following areas: Small arms, Rockets and missiles, Ammunition and propellants, Weapons systems maintenance, and Optronics

[2319] In order to provide comprehensive logistical support, this autonomous state-owned company has the necessary infrastructure, technology, and specialized human resources

[2320] FAMAE is integrated into the Army's conception of the defense industry

[2321] A central component of this concept is the Military Industry and Engineering Command (CIM), which also includes the Military Geographic Institute (IGM) and the Research and Control Institute (IDIC

[2322] This allows the company to actively contribute to meeting Army demands, as part of the Integrated Force Support System (SIAF), by identifying operational and logistical solutions generated from its engineering and industrial strengths

[2323] Shipyards and Workshops of the Navy (ASMAR) As regards the Navy, it has the Shipyards and Workshops of the Navy (ASMAR), an autonomous State company whose main function is to repair, careen and build the units of the Chilean Navy, being able, with its surplus capacity and without affecting its main function, to attend to the repair, careen and construction of national or foreign vessels

[2324] ASMAR concentrates its activities in four areas: Services: primarily aimed at calibrating instruments in the electrical, dimensional, pressure, time and frequency areas

[2325] Shipbuilding: Among the projects developed and implemented are logistics support vessels, auxiliary vessels, and general service vessels

[2326] To date, it has built 105 ships and naval vessels

[2327] Maintenance and Repair of Naval Units: offers comprehensive services to all types of vessels, in the areas of structures, electricity, electronics, optics, diesel engines, metallurgy, machine tools, and carpentry

[2328] • Recovery, Modernization, and Conversion of Units: Among the most significant recovery projects are the conversion and modernization work carried out on destroyers, frigates, missile boats, and submarines

[2329] Due to its activities, ASMAR is strategically located and has plants in Valparaíso, Talcahuano, and Punta Arenas

[2330] It also owns a 50% stake in the company "Sistemas de Defensa" (SISDEF), dedicated to systems integration and the development of command, control, communications, and IT systems, and a 50% stake in the Ibero-American Society of Naval Repairs (SOCIBER), which specializes in ship repair and refurbishment

[2331] These companies were created to complement ASMAR's activities in their respective fields, in partnership with internationally recognized European companies

[2332] National Aeronautics Company (ENAER) For its part, the Air Force's contribution is channeled primarily through the National Aeronautics Company

[2333] This autonomous state-owned company was created in 1984 in response to the Chilean Air Force's need for an organization that could meet its demands for maintenance, repair, and manufacturing of aircraft, parts, and components

[2334] The company's products and services include training aircraft manufacturing, aircraft assembly manufacturing, special parts manufacturing, aircraft modernization, aircraft maintenance, engine maintenance and repair, and accessory repair

[2335] The company has established strategic business alliances, both nationally and internationally, for various projects

[2336] In terms of manufacturing, the “Pillán” basic training aircraft is distinctive, and is in full operation in several air forces around the world

[2337] In the manufacturing of parts and components, co-productions with prestigious companies from different countries in the aeronautical world are notable

[2338] Finally, regarding modifications, ENAER has incorporated cutting-edge technology into its combat aircraft, and its installed capacity allows it to perform depot maintenance of its own aircraft material and that of military and civilian aircraft from other countries

[2339] Defense companies contribute to national defense through their technical and industrial capabilities, meeting the maximum demands of the Chilean Armed Forces

[2340] They have also promoted the development of science and technology in the military field with many applications in the civilian sector, constituting a source of innovation

[2341] While FAMAE is geared toward providing services to all military institutions, there is a strategic decision to direct its activities toward meeting the challenges of maintenance and technological development of the new weapons systems incorporated into the Army

[2342] For its part, ASMAR has prioritized its relationship with the Chilean Navy, responding to the fleet's requirements and the challenges inherent to shipbuilding and maintenance in an increasingly competitive national and international market

[2343] For its part, ENAER has tended to cater to the general aeronautical market, both civil and commercial, as well as military, although it is likely to refocus its emphasis in light of the long-term impacts on the market generated by the international financial crisis that erupted in

[2344] In general, defense companies constitute technological platforms for the research, development, production, and maintenance of weapons systems and equipment, seeking to transfer their capabilities to the place of use of their products

[2345] To meet its challenges, the defense industry has developed or incorporated significant engineering capabilities with specialized professionals and technicians

[2346] It has also had to renew its production capacities and improve its management to focus on competitive markets

[2347] In addition to state-owned companies dedicated to the defense industry, there are several private companies in the country, which have emerged in the last three decades, whose main focus is the development, repair, and maintenance of defense-related systems

[2348] The private sector that has seen the greatest development in the country is electronics, including sensors, fire control systems, electronic warfare systems, and command and control systems

[2349] The existence of these types of companies, whose main activity is providing services to the Armed Forces, contributes to the development of new technologies in the country and, consequently, increases the degree of national autonomy in their areas of expertise

[2350] FINANCIAL AND APPLIED RESOURCES FOR DEFENSE The defense policy contains a series of elements that allow its institutions, taking advantage of their individual and collective strengths, to contribute to meeting the social needs of the national community and environmental sustainability

[2351] This means that the armed forces must use some of the financial, material, and human resources they are provided with to achieve these goals

[2352] Certainly, it can be expected that public concern over these matters will grow in the future

[2353] RESOURCES APPLIED IN SOCIAL AND ENVIRONMENTAL MATTERS

[2354] In the Social Political Dimension of Social Responsibility of National Defense, the debate surrounding equity and growth has progressively incorporated the concept of sustainability as a necessary condition for these processes to be sustained over time

[2355] In other words, it's impossible to think about development without it being sustainable; it's no longer just about economic development, but also social and environmental development

[2356] The paradigm that encompasses this new perspective is known today as Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR

[2357] It is in this conceptual context that the contribution that the armed forces make to the country's development is now proposed to be inserted

[2358] The Armed Forces have consistently carried out actions to benefit the community; however, these actions are largely unknown or only known and appreciated in times of disaster

[2359] Hence the need to highlight this contribution to the country by placing it within a framework that systematizes and coordinates it

[2360] This effort was embodied in 2008 with the Ministry of National Defense's Corporate Social Responsibility Policy, whose main objective is to guide and frame all institutional actions that contribute to the country's sustainable development

[2361] These actions contribute to sustainable development in social, economic and environmental matters, as well as to strengthening social cohesion and promoting high standards of organizational management

[2362] Within the framework of national defense, the contribution to the country's social cohesion is realized through activities and actions that benefit social sectors excluded from comprehensive development and that promote citizen participation and interaction with the community

[2363] • The contribution of advocacy to sustainability refers to all the actions and activities that institutions develop and that are aimed at meeting different needs in the environmental, economic, and social spheres

[2364] • In the area of organizational management, policies, programs, instruments, or actions are considered such as the following: good labor practices, modernization of the management process, inclusion of respect for human rights in institutional doctrines, support for staff with regard to their reintegration into the civilian workforce upon retirement, and transparency mechanisms regarding expenditure and investment

[2365] In short, the Ministry of Defense's Corporate Social Responsibility refers both to the active contribution to the social, economic, and environmental progress of the community, primarily through the participation of its dependent institutions, and to a permanent and progressive expression of its principles within the Armed Forces

[2366] Social Responsibility and the Armed Forces The institutions of the Armed Forces assume the social responsibility of defense as a contribution to the national effort to create the country's security conditions

[2367] Basically, this contribution takes the form of direct community support and national development-oriented activities, including environmental protection

[2368] a. Direct support to the community In order to contribute to national unity and cohesion

[2369] The Armed Forces provide direct support to citizens

[2370] This consists of a series of activities that, together, promote the strengthening of trust, civic friendship, civic responsibility, the cultivation of patriotic spirit, and the preservation of the nation's historical and cultural heritage

[2371] These activities take on a particularly significant meaning when the Armed Forces come to support the community in emergencies and natural disasters

[2372] b. Contribution to national development As part of the social responsibility of defense, the Armed Forces carry out various tasks contributing to the development of the country, which are expressed in physical works and scientific-technological tasks

[2373] Its activities in road, port and airport infrastructure are well known

[2374] Likewise, their presence in Antarctica is significant, where they not only carry out activities supporting science but also tasks and actions aimed at strengthening Chile's presence on the continent

[2375] In short, these are activities that effectively help integrate the territory, maintain national sovereignty, and facilitate industrial and technological development

[2376] In the Environmental Dimension Environmental Policy of Defense The general objective of the environmental policy of the Chilean government is to promote the environmental sustainability of the development process and its ultimate goal is to improve the quality of life of Chileans and future generations

[2377] Each sector of the State must develop an environmental policy applicable to its own area, within the framework established by national policy

[2378] To this end, the Ministry of National Defense has formulated a sectoral policy, called the Defense Environmental Policy, which will serve as a general guideline for the specific policies of the various agencies and institutions under the Ministry

[2379] The legal framework within which the national defense institutions operate in this area is Law No. 19,300, "Constitutional Organic Law on General Environmental Foundations," and the international declarations, conventions, and treaties signed by the State of Chile. To organize the Ministry's activities in this area, the National Defense Environmental Committee is in place, and there is an Environmental Cooperation Agreement between the National Environmental Commission (CONAMA) and the Ministry of Defense

[2380] Its objectives are to establish a collaborative and working relationship between both institutions, to make defense policy environmentally sound, to identify the environmental issues it must address, and to integrate the Armed Forces and Law Enforcement into actions that provide concrete solutions to environmental problems

[2381] BOX 37: SPECIFIC OBJECTIVES OF DEFENSE ENVIRONMENTAL POLICY • Institutional development of environmental policy and management instruments. • Strengthen the technical capacity of defense institution personnel in environmental management. • Strengthen institutional participation in the national environmental management system and international outreach

[2382] The environmental activities carried out by national defense institutions can be grouped into two broad areas: • First, those related to the environmental sustainability of the activities of the organizations that comprise the defense sector; these organizations have progressively incorporated procedures to minimize the environmental effects generated by the normal performance of their activities

[2383] • Secondly, the participation of national defense institutions in the State's National Environmental Management System (SNGAE

[2384] The current national environmental institutions assign specific obligations and responsibilities to certain national defense institutions regarding the control, prevention, and care of the environment in Chile or in areas where the State has international commitments to contribute its resources to environmental preservation

[2385] The Armed Forces and the Environment a. Army The Army promotes actions aimed at promoting a better quality of life and using natural resources rationally, through its environmental policy, which is manifested and expressed through activities, highlighting, among others, the following: • The operation of the Army Environmental Management System (SGAE); • Active participation in compliance with environmental legislation, acting in coordination with public agencies, academic entities, private companies and non-governmental organizations, in order to protect, preserve and conserve the ecosystems under the responsibility of the Army; • The rational and sustainable use of natural resources

[2386] The institution's technical advisory body on the environment is the Army's "Risk Prevention and Environment Department" (PRYMAE), of the Logistics Division

[2387] Its mission is to advise, plan, implement, and monitor the Army's activities in the areas of risk prevention and the environment, specifically in the professionalization of the management of risks inherent to the military profession

[2388] The institutional environmental policy, in effect since October 29, 2004, establishes that the Army must develop and support measures aimed at preserving a healthy, pollution-free environment through its activities and through contributions to other related organizations

[2389] Among the internal regulations that govern the Army's environmental activities, the following stand out: The "Army Risk Prevention and Environmental Provisions"; The "Army Solid and Recyclable Waste Management Plan and Solid Waste Management Manual"; The "Army Risk Prevention and Environmental Regulations"; "Biodiversity Impact Management" Another way to address environmental challenges is the application of ISO 9001, 14001 and OHSAS 18001 standards, with the aim of improving the quality of the Army's operations

[2390] In the use and management of natural resources, the Army implements energy efficiency programs that save institutional resources

[2391] This action includes the use of renewable resources (water, wind, solar, etc.), requiring the institution's units to use this type of energy in infrastructure and in the construction of new barracks and facilities

[2392] Finally, we are collaborating with the "Sendero de Chile" program, one of the projects commemorating the bicentennial of Chile's independence, promoted by the National Environmental Commission (CONAMA), the National Forestry Corporation (CONAF), regional governments, and other public and private organizations

[2393] b. Navy In 2002, the Navy issued its environmental policy, which aims to implement legal regulations for environmental protection through actions carried out within the institution as well as externally

[2394] The "Internal Naval Action Front" is led by the Directorate General of Naval Services and encompasses environmental activities within the institution, which encompass its members, ships, and land infrastructure

[2395] On the “Home Front”, the following activities stand out

[2396] Studies and Projects • In floating units: replacement of the wastewater and garbage discharge system; installation of oil-water separation plants, water treatment plants; and garbage crushers and compactors. • Research project called "ISO 14001/2000 Model Environmental Management System at the Almirante Nef Naval Hospital," consisting of implementing the aforementioned management system at the aforementioned hospital, in accordance with the aforementioned ISO standard. • Registry of hazardous substances in use by the institution, to create, from it, instructions for their storage and transportation

[2397] Environmental Management in Land-Based Infrastructure The Navy carries out activities on its land-based facilities related to environmental conservation, pollution prevention, emissions and waste treatment, and the repair and compensation of environmental damage

[2398] Network of environmental stations It is made up of a total of 19 permanent stations, whose applications and uses are the following: • Support for scientific and field activities by recording environmental and oceanographic parameters

[2399] • Monitoring the “El Niño and Southern Oscillation” (ENSO) phenomenon

[2400] El Niño Research Cruises

[2401] These cruises will conduct water column sampling at a total of 22 stations located off the central coast of Chile, where temperature, salinity, and dissolved oxygen data will be recorded down to a depth of 1,500 meters

[2402] Oceanographic cruises are designed to study oceanographic, meteorological, marine biodiversity and underwater morphology parameters in remote geographical areas

[2403] In relation to the "External Maritime Action Front", the Navy, through the General Directorate of Maritime Territory and Merchant Marine, in its role as supervisory authority, carries out actions aimed at enforcing the precepts established by the numerous national and international legal bodies, ratified by Chile, relating to the prevention, reduction and control of pollution, especially those referring to marine pollution and the protection of the aquatic environment and its resources

[2404] To this end, the maritime authority carries out preventive activities aimed at assessing the environmental status of water bodies under national jurisdiction and maintains personnel, equipment, and materials to combat pollution, always ready to control a disaster at any point along our coastline

[2405] These activities include the following: Implementation of a plan to monitor the environmental conditions of the aquatic environment

[2406] • Action against pollution of the aquatic environment in coordination with the National Environmental Commission (CONAMA) and implementation of necessary actions to preserve and mitigate the effects of hydrocarbon spills in water bodies. • Maintenance and updating of data records on emission sources in continental Chile, insular Chile, and the Chilean Antarctic Territory

[2407] • Meteorological support to civilian and military units in maritime territory

[2408] c. Air Force Environmental activities in the Chilean Air Force are managed according to an Institutional Environmental Policy (PAI), which is based on the following principles: • Harmonization of the systemic development of the institution with the national environmental policy

[2409] • Fulfillment of the institutional mission by incorporating specialized capabilities and best environmental practices. • Respect for renewable and non-renewable resources in accordance with the concept of sustainability. • Commitment to preventing damage caused to the environment by the institution's own activities and to repair it when appropriate. • Responsibility to the national and international community, persevering in the recognition, adoption, and compliance with current environmental regulations, as well as with the international agreements, treaties, and conventions signed by Chile

[2410] Based on the PAI, the Air Force has incorporated environmental criteria and procedures aimed at protecting the environment, particularly noise pollution

[2411] The FACH's Meteorology Directorate is particularly important in providing information on various environmental variables, contributing to the country's environmental management and, in particular, to the rapid response to environmental emergencies

[2412] This agency is also responsible for managing the Global Warming monitoring stations in Chile (in Valdivia and Easter Island

[2413] GREEN AIRPORTS The new concept of “Green Airports” implies that air terminals must have a series of standards that are not invasive to the environment in which they are located

[2414] The International Civil Aviation Organization, in its Environmental Protection Guidance Manual, notes that since airports are large facilities, they interfere with the environment, particularly with the structure of urban space and its social and economic relations. It therefore recommends that aviation authorities mitigate their effects as much as possible

[2415] • In this regard, the aeronautical authorities established the environmental objective of “promoting the environmental sustainability of the development process of the Chilean Aerospace System and thereby ensuring the continuous improvement of the community's quality of life.”

[2416] The DGAC has established specific environmental policies, including the implementation of an Environmental Management System through which the regulations issued by the environmental authority will be structured, documented, and adjusted

[2417] • In the field of certification, in 2006 the environmental goal was established to achieve ISO 14.001:2004 certification for an airfield managed by the DGAC, which was achieved with Carriel Sur in Concepción in 2008, chosen for having regular air traffic, a high technological level and for serving as an alternative to the Arturo Merino Benítez Airport (AMB

[2418] For the period 2008-2012, the management goal was to certify the main airports in the network managed by the DGAC to ISO

[2419] • In the area of environmental compliance and as a result of the implementation of the second runway project for AMB Airport, a series of measures were implemented to protect the natural environment of the airport and minimize the impacts generated by its operation

[2420] Similarly, the Air Force is working on a plan to remodel the Presidente Frei Air Base and adapt it to the use of clean and alternative energy

[2421] These works are intended to turn Frei Base into the first "green base" in our country

[2422] The plan also includes the installation of a wastewater treatment plant (a project already completed) and considers the installation of a winddiesel turbine system that will provide 80% of the energy required for electricity and heating, which will place Frei Base among the bases with the highest environmental standards in Antarctica

[2423] In parallel, the Ministry of Public Works and the General Directorate of Civil Aeronautics are working on the establishment of “green airports”

[2424] The objective of this program is to achieve certification under the ISO 14000 environmental standard, leading to a sustainable design focused primarily on energy savings and the efficient use of non-renewable resources

[2425] This concept will allow these buildings to be completely environmentally friendly and fully sustainable in the long term
